reverse‑bafu · 58 of 101 system‑terminated datasets rebuilt

Rebuilt BAFU inventories

Aggregated BAFU‑2026 datasets rebuilt as unit processes with links into the existing database, each by a different route. Every rebuild is one JSON spec, run through resolve → calibrate → build → check; the pages below are those specs and check reports rendered.

Agreement is measured on the cumulative inventory itself — flow by flow, no impact assessment, so the verdict does not depend on a characterisation method. Three views of the same comparison: the 50 largest kilogram flows, the share of the original’s total kilogram mass that the rebuilt process reproduces within ±10 %, and every flow counted alike (which the long tail of small background flows dominates). The hybrid node of each equals the original flow for flow by construction. "Calibrated amounts" were fitted by bounded least squares with the input list fixed; inputs at a bound signal the report's range disagrees with the original vector.

Rebuilt · S3
12 inputs
2 direct flows
NOT S1. The cited report prints no inventory. The monomer is a mass balance (1 kg of PVC carries 1 kg of vinyl chloride; chain-growth polymerisation adds the monomer unchanged). The utility side - nitrogen blanketing, compressed air, process water, furnace gas, steam, electricity, auxiliary organics and the treatment of the unpolluted process water - is the input list of BAFU's own unit process 'Emulsion polymerisation, polyvinylchlorid' [RER], with its amounts as the centre of 0.5-2x bounds. Caveat, stated rather than hidden: that module is generic. Its one consumer in BAFU-2026 is 'Polyvinylalcohol, at plant' (1.94 kg of it), and its monomer slot is filled with 1.355 kg of dichloromethane, which is not a PVC reagent. Only its utility rows are used here; the monomer slot is replaced by vinyl chloride. That is why this is S3 with a template for the utilities, not S2.

Transport, freight, lorry, fleet averageRERcalibrated
The dataset's own text names 'transportation of feedstock' but no report in the bundle gives a distance for it, so the amount is calibrated. The starting value is the ecoinvent v2 standard distance for precursor materials (Althaus 2007 sec. 34.6.7: 'As there is no information about the transport amounts, standard distances and means according to Frischknecht et al. (2007) are used for the different raw materials') = 0.6 tkm rail + 0.1 tkm lorry per kg of precursor, the ratio BAFU's Ethylene dichloride, Ethylene oxide, Ethyl benzene and PET-amorphous unit processes all carry; precursor mass here 1 kg. It is left free rather than fixed because the PlasticsEurope eco-profile accounts for its feedstock transport as elementary flows only - it carries no vehicle or track infrastructure - so an ecoinvent transport dataset adds a background the target cannot have, and the fit is the right place to decide how much of it belongs.
2.50e-10ton kilometer0%1.16e-10
Transport, freight, railRERcalibrated
The dataset's own text names 'transportation of feedstock' but no report in the bundle gives a distance for it, so the amount is calibrated. The starting value is the ecoinvent v2 standard distance for precursor materials (Althaus 2007 sec. 34.6.7: 'As there is no information about the transport amounts, standard distances and means according to Frischknecht et al. (2007) are used for the different raw materials') = 0.6 tkm rail + 0.1 tkm lorry per kg of precursor, the ratio BAFU's Ethylene dichloride, Ethylene oxide, Ethyl benzene and PET-amorphous unit processes all carry; precursor mass here 1 kg. It is left free rather than fixed because the PlasticsEurope eco-profile accounts for its feedstock transport as elementary flows only - it carries no vehicle or track infrastructure - so an ecoinvent transport dataset adds a background the target cannot have, and the fit is the right place to decide how much of it belongs.

Chemical plant, organicsRERcalibrated · at lower bound
Rajabihamedani 2025 sec. 3.6: 'For the implementation, the dataset "Chemical plant, organics {RER}U" is used. The quantity is the standard amount for production of chemicals of 4.0E-10 p.' The target carries exactly that, which is why 4.0E-10 is the upper bound here. It is the upper bound and not the amount because ESU added one chemical plant per *dataset*, i.e. one for a whole cradle-to-gate chain, while this model links to a precursor dataset that already carries its own 4.0E-10 - so a second one is a double count against the target's own convention. That matters more than its size suggests: of the 1790 flows in the cumulative vector of every one of these 14 datasets, 1316 get more than 90 % of their value from this single exchange (see scripts/plasticseurope_infrastructure_diagnostic.py), so doubling it doubles three quarters of the vector. The amount is therefore fitted between 0 and the report's 4.0E-10.

Evidence

  • 2025 - LCI plastics - Rajabihamedani.pdf (the dataset's own cited report) — sec. 3.2, Tab. 1.1, sec. 6
    Prints no unit-process inventory for any of its 14 datasets: 'All datasets addressed for the updates are nowadays only available as system processes and therefore are modelled accordingly in this study' (sec. 3.2); Foreground data = 'Cumulative inventories of Plastics as provided by PlasticsEurope' (Tab. 1.1). Verified page by page, including every table that is embedded as an image. The full finding is in specs/evidence/<code>/response-0-locate.json. What the report does give and is used here: sec. 3.6 (infrastructure, 4.0E-10 p of Chemical plant, organics).
  • bafu-2026 ecoSpold metadata of the target (includedProcesses / technology) — includedProcesses / technology
    'The polymerisation process, upstream processes like ethylene, chlorine, and VCM production, electricity, and steam production as well as transportation of feedstock and waste treatment.'
  • bafu-2026: Emulsion polymerisation, polyvinylchlorid [RER] (unit process, 9 inputs) — template for the utility side only
    Per kg: nitrogen, liquid 0.00125 kg; compressed air >30kW 8 bar 1.732 m3; tap water 2.475 kg; natural gas burned in industrial furnace 1MWth 0.885 MJ; steam for chemical processes 1.395 kg; electricity medium voltage 0.3764 kWh; chemicals organic 0.025 kg; treatment, sewage, unpolluted class 3 0.002475 m3; dichloromethane 1.355 kg (the monomer slot, not used); direct flows Water To Cooling 0.03139 m3 and Water to water 0.02982 m3.

Rebuilt · S3
9 inputs
0 direct flows
Chained variant: identical to specs/05a10e4b-polystyrene-expandable-at-plant.json except that the styrene input points at the rebuilt styrene node (76aac688-8d44-3337-ac3e-9cac4cdafd8b-disagg) in the sandbox instead of at BAFU's aggregated 'Styrene, at plant'. Its only purpose is to measure what 'terminate on the database' costs or buys when the ancestor stops being a black box: the aggregated styrene is a PlasticsEurope eco-profile with ~144 substances and no infrastructure, the rebuilt one is ethylbenzene dehydrogenation running through the full ecoinvent background with ~1670. Read the two check reports side by side; neither is 'the answer'. The reviewer decides which the export should ship - this variant is deliberately a separate file so that the committed spec is left exactly as its author wrote it. -- original note: NOT S1. The cited report prints no inventory; what it says about EPS is an error correction (sec. 3.8, the rejected 28.5 kg of natural gas per kg). The input list here comes from the dataset's own ecoSpold technology field, which for EPS is unusually explicit and names, in order: styrene monomer, organic peroxide initiators, an aqueous phase, flame retardant and elemental carbon, suspension stabilisers, chain transfer agents, expanding aids, nucleating agents, plasticisers, pentane as the blowing agent, heating to 80-150 C, a centrifuge, drying and sieving, and a coating step. Amounts for none of them are printed anywhere in the bundle, so styrene is fixed by mass balance and everything else is fitted. The flame retardant and the elemental carbon of grey EPS are left out on purpose: no dataset is named for either and proposing one would be guessing - see gaps in the comment. RESULT OF THE COMPARISON. Chaining changes almost nothing: 24 of 1670 determined flows within +-10 % (1 %, median |delta| 103.0 %) against the committed spec's 20 of 1671 (1 %, median 100.0 %). This is worth recording because the dependency chain of the 101 lists expandable polystyrene among the rebuilds that 'still terminate on an aggregated ancestor', with styrene as the blocker. Styrene is now rebuilt, so the blocker is gone - and the agreement does not move. Removing an aggregated ancestor is a transparency gain, not a numerical one; the 1 % here has a different cause, the sub-compartment split between the 2025 PlasticsEurope implementation of EPS and the ecoinvent-v2 nomenclature of styrene, documented in the PlasticsEurope section of exports/README.md.

Transport, freight, lorry, fleet averageRERcalibrated
The dataset's own text names 'transportation of feedstock' but no report in the bundle gives a distance for it, so the amount is calibrated. The starting value is the ecoinvent v2 standard distance for precursor materials (Althaus 2007 sec. 34.6.7: 'As there is no information about the transport amounts, standard distances and means according to Frischknecht et al. (2007) are used for the different raw materials') = 0.6 tkm rail + 0.1 tkm lorry per kg of precursor, the ratio BAFU's Ethylene dichloride, Ethylene oxide, Ethyl benzene and PET-amorphous unit processes all carry; precursor mass here 1 kg. It is left free rather than fixed because the PlasticsEurope eco-profile accounts for its feedstock transport as elementary flows only - it carries no vehicle or track infrastructure - so an ecoinvent transport dataset adds a background the target cannot have, and the fit is the right place to decide how much of it belongs.

1.96e-07ton kilometer0%1.56e-08
Chemical plant, organicsRERcalibrated · at lower bound
Rajabihamedani 2025 sec. 3.6: 'For the implementation, the dataset "Chemical plant, organics {RER}U" is used. The quantity is the standard amount for production of chemicals of 4.0E-10 p.' The target carries exactly that, which is why 4.0E-10 is the upper bound here. It is the upper bound and not the amount because ESU added one chemical plant per *dataset*, i.e. one for a whole cradle-to-gate chain, while this model links to a precursor dataset that already carries its own 4.0E-10 - so a second one is a double count against the target's own convention. That matters more than its size suggests: of the 1790 flows in the cumulative vector of every one of these 14 datasets, 1316 get more than 90 % of their value from this single exchange (see scripts/plasticseurope_infrastructure_diagnostic.py), so doubling it doubles three quarters of the vector. The amount is therefore fitted between 0 and the report's 4.0E-10.

Evidence

  • 2025 - LCI plastics - Rajabihamedani.pdf (the dataset's own cited report) — sec. 3.2, Tab. 1.1, sec. 6
    Prints no unit-process inventory for any of its 14 datasets: 'All datasets addressed for the updates are nowadays only available as system processes and therefore are modelled accordingly in this study' (sec. 3.2); Foreground data = 'Cumulative inventories of Plastics as provided by PlasticsEurope' (Tab. 1.1). Verified page by page, including every table that is embedded as an image. The full finding is in specs/evidence/<code>/response-0-locate.json. What the report does give and is used here: sec. 3.6 (infrastructure, 4.0E-10 p of Chemical plant, organics).
  • bafu-2026 ecoSpold metadata of the target (technology) — technology
    'Expandable Polystyrene (EPS) is produced by polymerisation of styrene monomer, a chain-growth reaction which is mostly initiated by free radical organic initiators. ... Styrene charged with organic peroxide initiators is added to an aqueous phase and forms a suspension upon stirring. Flame retardant and elemental carbon, as well as auxiliaries such as suspension stabilisers, chain transfer agents, expanding aids, nucleating agents and plasticisers can also be added. The styrene droplets polymerise to polystyrene during heating of the reactor between 80 and 150 C. Blowing agent, typically pentane, is added to the reactor during polymerisation and dissolves in the polymer, producing the Expandable Polystyrene bead. After the reactor is cooled, the polymer is separated from the water phase using a centrifuge. The EPS beads are dried and sieved into the required size fractions before being coated with additive'
  • 2025 - LCI plastics - Rajabihamedani.pdf — sec. 3.8 Obvious errors corrected in PE data
    'According to the latest PlasticsEurope data, methane emissions were first estimated for Polystyrene expandable seven times higher (0.226 kg vs. 0.031 kg) than previously estimated in UVEK 2025. This discrepancy is largely due to the significant consumption of natural gas - 28.5 kg - required to produce just 1 kg of polystyrene. Since such a high input is unrealistic a correction has been made.' A rejected figure, so it cannot be used as an amount; it is recorded because it is the only process-energy number the report ever prints for this dataset, and because it means the published vector for EPS carries a hand-made correction of unstated size.

Rebuilt · S3
9 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. The cited report prints no inventory; what it says about EPS is an error correction (sec. 3.8, the rejected 28.5 kg of natural gas per kg). The input list here comes from the dataset's own ecoSpold technology field, which for EPS is unusually explicit and names, in order: styrene monomer, organic peroxide initiators, an aqueous phase, flame retardant and elemental carbon, suspension stabilisers, chain transfer agents, expanding aids, nucleating agents, plasticisers, pentane as the blowing agent, heating to 80-150 C, a centrifuge, drying and sieving, and a coating step. Amounts for none of them are printed anywhere in the bundle, so styrene is fixed by mass balance and everything else is fitted. The flame retardant and the elemental carbon of grey EPS are left out on purpose: no dataset is named for either and proposing one would be guessing - see gaps in the comment.

Rebuilt · S3
6 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory for this dataset. The input list comes from the dataset's own ecoSpold technology field, 'production by different types of polymerization out of MMA'. The field names the monomer outright; 'sheet' is the cast form. Chain-growth (addition) polymerisation: the repeat unit and the monomer have the same empirical formula, so 1 kg of polymer carries 1 kg of monomer plus whatever is lost. No conversion-loss figure exists anywhere in the bundle, so 1.000 kg is used and the true value can only be higher - fixed, not fitted. Same convention as the committed HDPE / LDPE / LLDPE / PP / PVC specs. The monomer is itself an unflagged PlasticsEurope eco-profile (results/system_terminated_extended.csv, detected_by=structure), so this model terminates on an aggregated dataset and the structural check says so - correctly. It is linked to the BAFU dataset rather than to the sibling rebuild so that this spec's check measures only this spec's evidence; re-point it with the input's `sandbox` field to chain the rebuilds together. The BAFU datasets for beads and for sheet carry the same technology text and differ only in their cumulative vectors, and nothing in the bundle says how the two forms differ in process, so the two specs differ only in what the fit puts into the free utilities. That is stated here rather than hidden.

NOT S1. The cited report prints no inventory. The input list is the one the BAFU dataset itself declares in its ecoSpold technology/includedProcesses field, and the monomer amount is a mass balance, not a measurement: addition (chain-growth) polymerisation adds the monomer to the chain unchanged, so the repeat unit and the monomer have the same empirical formula and 1 kg of polymer needs 1 kg of monomer plus whatever is lost. No loss figure exists anywhere in the bundle, so 1.000 kg is used and the real value can only be higher - this is the one place where the model is knowingly at a bound. Electricity and steam are named by the dataset and quantified nowhere, so they are fitted, unbounded; they are the whole gate-to-gate difference between the polymer and its monomer. 'Propylene, at plant [RER]' is not one of the 101, so the harness does not flag it, but it is not a transparent unit process either: it carries 8 technosphere exchanges, all of them waste-treatment services, and 143 elementary flows - the same APME/PlasticsEurope shape as the datasets being rebuilt here. The chain therefore still terminates on aggregated data one level down, and saying so is part of the result.

Rajabihamedani 2025 sec. 3.6: 'For the implementation, the dataset "Chemical plant, organics {RER}U" is used. The quantity is the standard amount for production of chemicals of 4.0E-10 p.' The target carries exactly that, which is why 4.0E-10 is the upper bound here. It is the upper bound and not the amount because ESU added one chemical plant per *dataset*, i.e. one for a whole cradle-to-gate chain, while this model links to a precursor dataset that already carries its own 4.0E-10 - so a second one is a double count against the target's own convention. That matters more than its size suggests: of the 1790 flows in the cumulative vector of every one of these 14 datasets, 1316 get more than 90 % of their value from this single exchange (see scripts/plasticseurope_infrastructure_diagnostic.py), so doubling it doubles three quarters of the vector. The amount is therefore fitted between 0 and the report's 4.0E-10.

0.00308kilowatt hour31%0.00137
Transport, freight, lorry, fleet averageRERcalibrated
The dataset's own text names 'transportation of feedstock' but no report in the bundle gives a distance for it, so the amount is calibrated. The starting value is the ecoinvent v2 standard distance for precursor materials (Althaus 2007 sec. 34.6.7: 'As there is no information about the transport amounts, standard distances and means according to Frischknecht et al. (2007) are used for the different raw materials') = 0.6 tkm rail + 0.1 tkm lorry per kg of precursor, the ratio BAFU's Ethylene dichloride, Ethylene oxide, Ethyl benzene and PET-amorphous unit processes all carry; precursor mass here 1 kg. It is left free rather than fixed because the PlasticsEurope eco-profile accounts for its feedstock transport as elementary flows only - it carries no vehicle or track infrastructure - so an ecoinvent transport dataset adds a background the target cannot have, and the fit is the right place to decide how much of it belongs.

    Cumulative energy demand, this study: Polypropylene 75.62 MJ/kg. Propylene is not one of the 14 datasets of the report, so Tab. 5.3 gives no monomer figure to subtract and there is no cross-check on the fitted process energy for this one. Quoted as a sanity check on the fitted electricity and steam, and deliberately not used to set or bound them: it is an impact result, and no impact assessment enters this pipeline.

basis: per kg of hydrogen cyanide, Tab. 43.2 / 43.3 / 43.4 of chapter 43 of ecoinvent report No. 8 (Althaus et al. 2007), all printed per kg or per kg in grams; allocation: None stated. The chapter reports three co-products per kg HCN - 0.23 kg hydrogen (BMA route, if not burnt), 0.34 kg ammonium sulfate sold as fertiliser and 0.02 kg sodium cyanide - but gives no allocation factor, so every burden below stays with the HCN.. Review every derivation; unreviewed entries have reviewed_by = null. ON THE STRATEGY LABEL: the assembler writes S1 for everything it produces, and here S1 is right - every amount is a cell of Tab. 43.2, 43.3 or 43.4 of chapter 43, transcribed with its quote. Two caveats that S1 does not carry: the process-fuel line is a printed *range* (0.51-0.78 kg of natural gas, the hydrogen-combustion and hydrogen-export routes), so it is the one free amount and the fit left it at the lower bound; and the three co-products the chapter names (0.23 kg hydrogen, 0.34 kg ammonium sulfate sold as fertiliser, 0.02 kg sodium cyanide per kg of HCN) get no credit, because the chapter states no allocation.

Heat, natural gas, at industrial furnace 1MWCHcalibrated · at lower bound
Tab. 43.3 'Energy consumption for the production of HCN (kg/kg)' gives two columns, 'Hydrogen export route' 0.78 and 'Hydrogen combustion route' 0.51. The point estimate is the combustion route because Tab. 43.4's air emissions are stated to belong to it: 'These figures ... are calculated values based on the combustion of hydrogen gas in the process as a fuel that replaces part of the natural gas burned as a fuel.' The two columns are the printed range, so the amount is calibratable between them. Mapped to a heat-from-natural-gas dataset because BAFU has no 'natural gas burned' dataset in kg; the MJ of gas is charged as MJ of heat, which understates the gas by the furnace efficiency (~10 %). Flagged medium.

Rebuilt · S3
6 inputs
0 direct flows
Chained variant: identical to specs/1235438d-polystyrene-general-purpose-gpps-at-plant.json except that the styrene input points at the rebuilt styrene node (76aac688-8d44-3337-ac3e-9cac4cdafd8b-disagg) in the sandbox instead of at BAFU's aggregated 'Styrene, at plant'. Its only purpose is to measure what 'terminate on the database' costs or buys when the ancestor stops being a black box: the aggregated styrene is a PlasticsEurope eco-profile with ~144 substances and no infrastructure, the rebuilt one is ethylbenzene dehydrogenation running through the full ecoinvent background with ~1670. Read the two check reports side by side; neither is 'the answer'. The reviewer decides which the export should ship - this variant is deliberately a separate file so that the committed spec is left exactly as its author wrote it. -- NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory for this dataset. The input list comes from the dataset's own ecoSpold technology field, 'polymerization out of ethylene and benzene by free radical processes'. The field describes the chain back to the cracker ('out of ethylene and benzene'), which is the route to styrene; the monomer of general-purpose polystyrene is styrene and nothing else. Chain-growth (addition) polymerisation: the repeat unit and the monomer have the same empirical formula, so 1 kg of polymer carries 1 kg of monomer plus whatever is lost. No conversion-loss figure exists anywhere in the bundle, so 1.000 kg is used and the true value can only be higher - fixed, not fitted. Same convention as the committed HDPE / LDPE / LLDPE / PP / PVC specs. The monomer is itself an unflagged PlasticsEurope eco-profile (results/system_terminated_extended.csv, detected_by=structure), so this model terminates on an aggregated dataset and the structural check says so - correctly. It is linked to the BAFU dataset rather than to the sibling rebuild so that this spec's check measures only this spec's evidence; re-point it with the input's `sandbox` field to chain the rebuilds together. AFTER THE FIT. All five utilities went to zero (electricity 5.5E-11 kWh, heat 6.9E-9 MJ, both transports ~1E-9 tkm, the plant 3.6E-21 units), so the node is exactly '1 kg of aggregated styrene' - which is what the evidence says and is the honest outcome. Its headline flows are close: fossil CO2 +29 %, methane -2 %, biogenic CO2 +7 %, SO2 -8 %, NOx -13 %, NMVOC -9 %. Its overall flow agreement is nevertheless 25 of 1667 (1 %), and the reason is not the chemistry: 'Styrene, at plant' declares 0.5615 kg of hazardous waste to incineration per kg, against 0.0070 kg for this dataset, 0.0088 for HIPS and 0.0022 for benzene. A polymer's cradle-to-gate profile cannot contain 80x less waste than the monomer it is made of, so one of the two numbers is wrong, and the mass balance of the rest of the family says it is the styrene one. Every flow that hazardous-waste chain feeds - calcite +1010 %, gravel +1120 %, rock salt +3094 %, chloride to water +4690 % - is that single exchange, not this model. See exports/README.md, 'The APME eco-profiles the type=2 flag missed'. RESULT OF THE COMPARISON. Chaining makes it worse, and that is the point of the measurement: 8 of 1668 determined flows within +-10 % (0 %, median |delta| 10123 %) against 25 of 1667 (1 %) when the same spec links BAFU's aggregated styrene. The reason is structural, not a defect of the styrene rebuild: the target is a 1990s industry survey that reports ~146 substances and no infrastructure, while the rebuilt styrene reaches ethylbenzene, benzene, the European grid and a chemical plant and therefore produces ~1670. 'Terminate on the database' buys transparency - the burden becomes traceable to ethylbenzene dehydrogenation - and costs agreement with a target whose background was never ecoinvent's. Anyone choosing between the two nodes should choose on that basis, not on the score.

Rebuilt · S3
8 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory. The dataset's ecoSpold technology field, 'product out of hydrogen cyanide and acetone', is the classic ACH route, and BAFU carries the intermediate as its own dataset ('xx Acetone cyanohydrin, at plant'), so the model is built one step at a time rather than collapsing the two: acetone + HCN -> ACH is the cyanohydrin spec, and this spec is the amidation/esterification step. C4H7NO (acetone cyanohydrin, 85.106) + H2SO4 (98.072) + CH3OH (methanol, 32.042) -> C5H8O2 (methyl methacrylate, 100.117) + NH4HSO4 (ammonium bisulfate, 115.103); mass balance 215.220 = 215.220. Per kg of MMA: 85.106/100.117 = 0.85006 kg of acetone cyanohydrin, 98.072/100.117 = 0.97957 kg of sulphuric acid and 32.042/100.117 = 0.32005 kg of methanol, with 1.14973 kg of ammonium bisulfate as co-product. Molar masses from the IUPAC 2021 standard atomic weights: H 1.008, C 12.011, N 14.007, O 15.999, S 32.06, Cl 35.45, Na 22.990. Amounts fixed, not fitted. The ammonium bisulfate co-product weighs more than the product itself and nothing in the bundle gives an allocation or says whether the plant regenerates the acid, so no credit is taken and that is the single largest uncertainty in this spec. The acetone cyanohydrin input is itself an unflagged eco-profile (rebuilt here), so the structural check flags a dependency.

Rebuilt · S3
7 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory. The dataset's ecoSpold technology field states the composition qualitatively - 'production out of hexamethylene diamine and adipic acid, filled with glass fibers' - and both components exist in BAFU: the unfilled polymer as an unflagged eco-profile and 'Glass fibre, at plant' [RER] as a real 20-input unit process. Compounding is a physical blend, so polymer + glass = 1.000 kg per kg of compound, which is imposed with mass_sum; the glass content is nowhere quantified in the bundle (commercial grades run 15-50 %), so the split is fitted, not measured, and is flagged low confidence. Note the circularity BAFU already contains and this spec inherits: 'Glass fibre, at plant' itself consumes 0.0283 kg of 'Nylon 6, at plant' as a sizing agent, so a glass-filled nylon depends on nylon twice. AFTER THE FIT - READ THIS BEFORE USING THE AMOUNTS. The fit put 0.9995 kg on the polymer and 2.3E-4 kg on the glass fibre, i.e. it modelled a glass-*filled* compound as unfilled polymer; commercial grades carry 15-50 % glass. The inventory agreement it buys is real (601 of 1669 determined flows within +-10 % (36 %), 20 of the 50 largest kilogram flows, 89 % of the kilogram mass, median |delta| 12.2 %) and the node is a fair reconstruction of the target's flows, but its composition is not a statement about the product and must not be quoted. The mechanism is the same one recorded on the ABS, SAN and HIPS specs: under a mass constraint the fit empties whichever candidate is an ecoinvent unit process - here 'Glass fibre, at plant', a 20-input dataset that drags a full ~1670-flow background into a target whose eco-profile reports ~145 substances - and keeps the candidate that belongs to the same APME family and therefore shares the target's waste-treatment chains. No bound was moved after the score was seen.

NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory. The dataset's ecoSpold technology field names three reagents - 'production out of phosgene, toluene and hydrogen' - which is the toluene -> dinitrotoluene -> toluenediamine -> TDI route and nothing else; hydrogen is only ever used to reduce the nitro groups, so the field implies the nitration and therefore the nitric acid, which is added here for that reason and flagged. Three steps. Nitration: C7H8 (toluene, 92.141) + 2 HNO3 (126.024) -> C7H6N2O4 (dinitrotoluene, 182.135) + 2 H2O (36.030); 218.165 = 218.165. Hydrogenation: C7H6N2O4 + 6 H2 (12.096) -> C7H10N2 (toluenediamine, 122.171) + 4 H2O (72.060); 194.231 = 194.231. Phosgenation: C7H10N2 + 2 COCl2 (197.820) -> C9H6N2O2 (TDI, 174.159) + 4 HCl (145.832); 319.991 = 319.991. Per kg of TDI: 92.141/174.159 = 0.52906 kg toluene, 126.024/174.159 = 0.72361 kg HNO3, 12.096/174.159 = 0.06945 kg hydrogen, 197.820/174.159 = 1.13585 kg phosgene; co-products 0.83733 kg HCl and 0.62064 kg water. Mass balance: 2.45797 kg in, 2.45797 kg out. Molar masses from the IUPAC 2021 standard atomic weights: H 1.008, C 12.011, N 14.007, O 15.999, S 32.06, Cl 35.45, Na 22.990. Amounts are fixed, not fitted: the stoichiometric floor at 100 % conversion. One input is an aggregated dataset - 'Toluene, liquid, at plant', itself an unflagged PlasticsEurope eco-profile whose own rebuild is blocked by the BTX allocation (see its locate response) - so the structural check flags a dependency, correctly. The sulphuric acid of the mixed-acid nitration is regenerated and is a recorded gap; so are the 0.837 kg of HCl co-product per kg of TDI, for which nothing in the bundle states an allocation. BAFU's nitric-acid dataset is declared per kg of HNO3, not of the 50 % solution - checked against 'Adipic acid, at plant', which carries 0.431 kg of it against a stoichiometric 0.862 kg, i.e. exactly half, consistent with the NOx recovery that route runs on.

Rebuilt · S3
6 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. The cited report prints no inventory. The input list is the one the BAFU dataset itself declares in its ecoSpold technology/includedProcesses field, and the monomer amount is a mass balance, not a measurement: addition (chain-growth) polymerisation adds the monomer to the chain unchanged, so the repeat unit and the monomer have the same empirical formula and 1 kg of polymer needs 1 kg of monomer plus whatever is lost. No loss figure exists anywhere in the bundle, so 1.000 kg is used and the real value can only be higher - this is the one place where the model is knowingly at a bound. Electricity and steam are named by the dataset and quantified nowhere, so they are fitted, unbounded; they are the whole gate-to-gate difference between the polymer and its monomer. Suspension polymerisation is carried out in an aqueous phase with a suspension stabiliser, and both the water and the stabiliser are gaps here: the dataset's own text names neither, and nothing in the bundle quantifies them. Only the monomer, the energy and the transport are modelled. Re-point the monomer to 99a93e93-...-disagg with the input's `sandbox` field to chain this onto the VCM rebuild.

0.00602kilogram14%0.00167
Transport, freight, lorry, fleet averageRERcalibrated
The dataset's own text names 'transportation of feedstock' but no report in the bundle gives a distance for it, so the amount is calibrated. The starting value is the ecoinvent v2 standard distance for precursor materials (Althaus 2007 sec. 34.6.7: 'As there is no information about the transport amounts, standard distances and means according to Frischknecht et al. (2007) are used for the different raw materials') = 0.6 tkm rail + 0.1 tkm lorry per kg of precursor, the ratio BAFU's Ethylene dichloride, Ethylene oxide, Ethyl benzene and PET-amorphous unit processes all carry; precursor mass here 1 kg. It is left free rather than fixed because the PlasticsEurope eco-profile accounts for its feedstock transport as elementary flows only - it carries no vehicle or track infrastructure - so an ecoinvent transport dataset adds a background the target cannot have, and the fit is the right place to decide how much of it belongs.

1.65e-04kilowatt hour0%7.36e-05
Chemical plant, organicsRERcalibrated · at lower bound
Rajabihamedani 2025 sec. 3.6: 'For the implementation, the dataset "Chemical plant, organics {RER}U" is used. The quantity is the standard amount for production of chemicals of 4.0E-10 p.' The target carries exactly that, which is why 4.0E-10 is the upper bound here. It is the upper bound and not the amount because ESU added one chemical plant per *dataset*, i.e. one for a whole cradle-to-gate chain, while this model links to a precursor dataset that already carries its own 4.0E-10 - so a second one is a double count against the target's own convention. That matters more than its size suggests: of the 1790 flows in the cumulative vector of every one of these 14 datasets, 1316 get more than 90 % of their value from this single exchange (see scripts/plasticseurope_infrastructure_diagnostic.py), so doubling it doubles three quarters of the vector. The amount is therefore fitted between 0 and the report's 4.0E-10.

Rebuilt · S3
8 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory. The dataset's own ecoSpold technology field is unusually specific - 'production by interfacial polycondesation out of phosgene and bisphenol A' - and names both reagents and the process variant, and BAFU carries a real unit process for each ('Bisphenol A, powder, at plant', 7 inputs; 'Phosgene, liquid, at plant', 7 inputs), so this model terminates on the database with no aggregated dependency at all. Amounts are the polycondensation stoichiometry: C15H16O2 (bisphenol A, 228.291) + COCl2 (phosgene, 98.910) -> -[C16H14O3]- (carbonate repeat unit, 254.285) + 2 HCl (72.916); mass balance 327.201 = 327.201. Per kg of polycarbonate: 228.291/254.285 = 0.89777 kg of bisphenol A and 98.910/254.285 = 0.38897 kg of phosgene, releasing 0.28675 kg of HCl. In the interfacial process the HCl is taken up by caustic soda, 2 NaOH per repeat unit = 2 x 39.997 / 254.285 = 0.31459 kg. Molar masses from the IUPAC 2021 standard atomic weights: H 1.008, C 12.011, N 14.007, O 15.999, S 32.06, Cl 35.45, Na 22.990. The caustic soda is not named by the technology field; it is included because the field says *interfacial* polycondensation, which by definition runs the bisphenol as its disodium salt in an aqueous phase against phosgene in dichloromethane, and the HCl released must be neutralised. Two named-but-unquantified pieces are left out and recorded as gaps: the dichloromethane solvent (recycled, so its net consumption is a make-up rate nothing prints) and the chain terminator/catalyst. All three amounts are fixed, not fitted: they are the stoichiometric floor at 100 % conversion, so the true values can only be higher.

The cited report (Rajabihamedani 2025) prints no unit process - it documents only the post-processing of a PlasticsEurope system process (see specs/evidence/582ad303-.../response-0-locate.json). The inventory here is transcribed from Althaus 2007, ecoinvent report No. 8, Tab. 35.3, which documents the multi-output process 'ethylene oxide, in ethylene glycol plant' that ecoinvent v2 used for this same dataset name, and allocated to MEG with the report's own 85 % price-times-mass factor over its 0.825 kg of MEG. The transcription is verified, not assumed: applying the same table with the DEG factor (7 %/0.1 kg) and the TEG factor (8 %/0.04 kg) reproduces BAFU's own 'Diethylene glycol, at plant' and 'Triethylene glycol, at plant' unit processes on all eleven exchanges the table covers - exactly on nine of them (ethylene oxide 0.4921 kg, electricity 0.2639 kWh, chemical plant 2.8E-10, waste heat 0.945 MJ, acetaldehyde 9.8E-07, ethylene 4.2E-06, water 0.0042 m3, and the TEG column 1.406 / 0.754 / 8E-10) and to 0.15 % on the two four-digit ones (COD/BOD 2.013E-04 against 2.016E-04, TOC/DOC 5.248E-05 against 5.250E-05). The twelfth exchange, the waterborne water emission, is not in Tab. 35.3 at all and is taken from the co-products' own 0.95 ratio. This is S2 and not S1 because the BAFU dataset being rebuilt is the PlasticsEurope 2019 eco-profile (reference year 2023), a different data generation from the 1997-2000 ecoinvent v2 inventory transcribed here; the structure is the same product's, the vintage is not. Steam is calibrated because Tab. 35.3 omits it and sec. 35.6.2 says so explicitly, and the infrastructure is calibrated between 0 and the transcribed 4.1212E-10 because the ethylene-oxide precursor already carries one chemical plant of its own.

Evidence

  • 2007 - LCI chemicals - Althaus.pdf — Tab. 35.3 (printed p. 307, PDF p. 388), ch. 35 Ethylene Glycol
    Multi-output process 'ethylene oxide, in ethylene glycol plant' [RER], per 1 kg: ethylene oxide, at plant 7.03E-1 kg; electricity, medium voltage, production UCTE, at grid 3.77E-1 kWh; chemical plant, organics 4.00E-10 unit; Water, unspecified natural origin 6.00E-03 m3; Heat, waste 1.35E+00 MJ; Acetaldehyde 1.40E-06 kg; Ethene 6.00E-06 kg; BOD5 2.88E-04 kg; COD 2.88E-01 kg (see note below); DOC 7.50E-05 kg; TOC 7.50E-05 kg. Outputs diethylene glycol 0.1 kg, ethylene glycol 0.825 kg, triethylene glycol 0.04 kg, with the allocation row 7 / 85 / 8 %. Every amount below is that column times 0.85/0.825 = 1.030303.
  • 2007 - LCI chemicals - Althaus.pdf — Tab. 35.2 and sec. 35.6.1
    'It is assumed that an average 82.5% of the glycols consists of MEG. This report assumes that a further 10% is DEG, the remainder being TEG and heavy glycols.' Tab. 35.2 gives Input: Ethylene oxide 703, Output: MEG 825, DEG 100, TEG 40, Heavy glycols 35 (kg), with price x mass allocation factors 85 / 7 / 8 %. Sec. 35.5 notes the wider IPPC range: 'the glycol products typically consists of 70 - 95% w/w of MEG' - the 82.5 % split is one point in it.
  • 2007 - LCI chemicals - Althaus.pdf — sec. 35.6.2 Energy usage
    'It must be assumed that some steam is utilized for glycol distillation. No figures are given. Electrical energy consumption is given as 0.377 kWh / kg in (BUWAL 232).' The steam input is therefore in the model but calibrated, unbounded - the report names it and refuses to quantify it.
  • bafu-2026: Diethylene glycol, at plant [RER] and Triethylene glycol, at plant [RER] — cross-check of the transcription
    Both are 3-input unit processes still in BAFU-2026 and are the co-products of the very table transcribed here. Tab. 35.3 x 0.07/0.1 gives ethylene oxide 0.4921 kg, electricity 0.2639 kWh, chemical plant 2.80E-10 unit, water 0.0042 m3, waste heat 0.945 MJ, acetaldehyde 9.8E-07, ethylene 4.2E-06, BOD 2.016E-04, COD 2.016E-04, DOC/TOC 5.25E-05 - which is exactly what BAFU's DEG carries (0.4921 / 0.2639 / 2.8E-10 / 0.0042 / 0.945 / 9.8E-07 / 4.2E-06 / 2.013E-04 / 2.013E-04 / 5.248E-05). Same for TEG at 0.08/0.04 (EO 1.406, electricity 0.754, plant 8E-10). Two consequences: the transcription is verified, and the report's COD of 2.88E-01 kg is a typo for 2.88E-04 (the co-products carry BOD = COD), which is what is used here. The co-products also fix the water balance, 0.00399/0.0042 = 0.95 of the water input leaving as a waterborne emission; Tab. 35.3 gives only the input.
  • 2025 - LCI plastics - Rajabihamedani.pdf — sec. 3.6 Infrastructure
    'For all datasets, the inventory regarding infrastructure contributions was checked and supplemented. For the implementation, the dataset "Chemical plant, organics {RER}U" is used. The quantity is the standard amount for production of chemicals of 4.0E-10 p.' The BAFU target indeed carries 4.0E-10 p, and Tab. 35.3 gives the same 4.00E-10 unit - the two vintages agree on this one input.

6 inputs

NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory for this dataset. The input list comes from the dataset's own ecoSpold technology field, 'graft-based polybutadiene - produced out of butadiene'. The field names the monomer outright. Chain-growth (addition) polymerisation: the repeat unit and the monomer have the same empirical formula, so 1 kg of polymer carries 1 kg of monomer plus whatever is lost. No conversion-loss figure exists anywhere in the bundle, so 1.000 kg is used and the true value can only be higher - fixed, not fitted. Same convention as the committed HDPE / LDPE / LLDPE / PP / PVC specs. The monomer is itself an unflagged PlasticsEurope eco-profile (results/system_terminated_extended.csv, detected_by=structure), so this model terminates on an aggregated dataset and the structural check says so - correctly. It is linked to the BAFU dataset rather than to the sibling rebuild so that this spec's check measures only this spec's evidence; re-point it with the input's `sandbox` field to chain the rebuilds together.

Rebuilt · S3
7 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory. The dataset's ecoSpold technology field says 'production by different polymerization processes out of its different monomers' - it asserts that the monomers are the input list but does not name them; the product name does: styrene-acrylonitrile copolymer. Both monomers exist in BAFU, acrylonitrile as a real 9-input unit process ('Acrylonitrile from Sohio process, at plant'), styrene only as an unflagged eco-profile. Addition copolymerisation loses no mass, so the two add up to 1.000 kg, which is imposed with mass_sum; the split is nowhere quantified in the bundle and is therefore fitted, not measured - flagged low confidence. The same caveat as HIPS applies: a good inventory fit here would not prove the split is right. AFTER THE FIT - READ THIS BEFORE USING THE AMOUNTS. The calibration was run and its result is structurally wrong, which is worth more than the spec was: the fit drove acrylonitrile to 1.2E-10 kg, i.e. zero, and put the whole kilogram on styrene - a styrene-acrylonitrile copolymer modelled as pure polystyrene. The inventory agreement it buys is poor anyway: 3 of 1668 determined flows within +-10 %, median |delta| 133 %. This is the identifiability trap the project's own calibration benchmark predicts (README sec. 6: 'a better inventory fit with the wrong structure'), caught here on real data rather than on a synthetic case. The mechanism is specific and repeatable: under a mass constraint, when one candidate monomer is another dataset of the same APME eco-profile family and another is an ecoinvent unit process, the fit will empty the ecoinvent one, because it drags in a full background of ~1670 elementary flows that the target's ~145-substance eco-profile simply does not have, and every one of them counts against the fit. And among same-family candidates the fit follows the waste-treatment exchanges, not the chemistry - which is why it prefers polybutadiene to styrene: 'Styrene, at plant' declares 0.5615 kg of hazardous waste to incineration per kg, 80x what its own polymer GPPS declares (0.0070 kg) and 250x its own feedstock benzene (0.0022 kg), so any node that links styrene inherits a waste chain the target does not have. Conclusion for a user: the node's *inventory* is a fair reconstruction of the target's flows, its *composition* is not a statement about the product, and the split must not be quoted. The amounts are left exactly as the fit produced them - no bound was moved after the score was seen.
→

Rebuilt · S3
6 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. The cited report prints no inventory. The input list is the one the BAFU dataset itself declares in its ecoSpold technology/includedProcesses field, and the monomer amount is a mass balance, not a measurement: addition (chain-growth) polymerisation adds the monomer to the chain unchanged, so the repeat unit and the monomer have the same empirical formula and 1 kg of polymer needs 1 kg of monomer plus whatever is lost. No loss figure exists anywhere in the bundle, so 1.000 kg is used and the real value can only be higher - this is the one place where the model is knowingly at a bound. Electricity and steam are named by the dataset and quantified nowhere, so they are fitted, unbounded; they are the whole gate-to-gate difference between the polymer and its monomer. HDPE is made by low-pressure catalytic polymerisation, LDPE by the high-pressure radical route and LLDPE by low-pressure copolymerisation with an alpha-olefin. The bundle documents none of that quantitatively, so the three specs differ only in what the fit puts into electricity and steam. The alpha-olefin comonomer of LLDPE (a few per cent of 1-butene/1-hexene) is a known gap: no dataset for it is named anywhere and folding it into the ethylene is the standard simplification.

Transport, freight, railRERcalibrated
The dataset's own text names 'transportation of feedstock' but no report in the bundle gives a distance for it, so the amount is calibrated. The starting value is the ecoinvent v2 standard distance for precursor materials (Althaus 2007 sec. 34.6.7: 'As there is no information about the transport amounts, standard distances and means according to Frischknecht et al. (2007) are used for the different raw materials') = 0.6 tkm rail + 0.1 tkm lorry per kg of precursor, the ratio BAFU's Ethylene dichloride, Ethylene oxide, Ethyl benzene and PET-amorphous unit processes all carry; precursor mass here 1 kg. It is left free rather than fixed because the PlasticsEurope eco-profile accounts for its feedstock transport as elementary flows only - it carries no vehicle or track infrastructure - so an ecoinvent transport dataset adds a background the target cannot have, and the fit is the right place to decide how much of it belongs.

Chemical plant, organicsRERcalibrated · at lower bound
Rajabihamedani 2025 sec. 3.6: 'For the implementation, the dataset "Chemical plant, organics {RER}U" is used. The quantity is the standard amount for production of chemicals of 4.0E-10 p.' The target carries exactly that, which is why 4.0E-10 is the upper bound here. It is the upper bound and not the amount because ESU added one chemical plant per *dataset*, i.e. one for a whole cradle-to-gate chain, while this model links to a precursor dataset that already carries its own 4.0E-10 - so a second one is a double count against the target's own convention. That matters more than its size suggests: of the 1790 flows in the cumulative vector of every one of these 14 datasets, 1316 get more than 90 % of their value from this single exchange (see scripts/plasticseurope_infrastructure_diagnostic.py), so doubling it doubles three quarters of the vector. The amount is therefore fitted between 0 and the report's 4.0E-10.

Rebuilt · S3
7 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. Althaus (2007) lists this dataset in Tab. 1.1 as 'Reported in: Part II', but the 957-page report has no acetone-cyanohydrin chapter - the index points at a chapter the report does not contain (see the locate response). The dataset's own ecoSpold technology field, 'production from acetone and hydrogen cyanide by base-catalyzed reaction', names both reagents and the catalysis. C3H6O (acetone, 58.080) + HCN (27.026) -> C4H7NO (acetone cyanohydrin, 85.106); a pure addition, 85.106 = 85.106, so the two reagents add up to exactly 1.000 kg per kg of product: 58.080/85.106 = 0.68244 kg of acetone and 27.026/85.106 = 0.31756 kg of hydrogen cyanide. Molar masses from the IUPAC 2021 standard atomic weights: H 1.008, C 12.011, N 14.007, O 15.999, S 32.06, Cl 35.45, Na 22.990. This is the one reaction in the whole set with no co-product and no yield ambiguity: it is a 1:1 addition and the masses close exactly, so the amounts are fixed. Both reagents are themselves unflagged eco-profiles in this same set - and both are rebuilt here from the Althaus report (acetone from the cumene figure, hydrogen cyanide from Tab. 43.2/43.3/43.4) - so this is the deepest chain in the set. It is linked to the BAFU datasets rather than to those rebuilds so that its check measures its own evidence; re-point with `sandbox` to chain them. The base catalyst is a gap: named, never quantified.

Rebuilt · S3
6 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory. The input list comes from the dataset's own ecoSpold technology field, 'mixture of process of dehydrogenation of ethyl benzene and oxirane process', and the amount from the dehydrogenation equation C6H5C2H5 -> C6H5CH=CH2 + H2: 106.168/104.152 = 1.01936 kg of ethylbenzene per kg of styrene at full conversion, with 0.01936 kg of hydrogen released. That is the stoichiometric floor - industrial reactors run 60-70 % conversion per pass with recycle, so the gross feed is higher but the net consumption per kg of product is this - and it is fixed, not fitted. The second route the field names, the oxirane (propylene-oxide/styrene-monomer) process, is not modelled: it is a two-product process whose share and allocation neither the field nor any bundled report gives, and folding it in would require inventing both. Said plainly: this model is the dehydrogenation route only. Dehydrogenation is strongly endothermic - about 1.2 MJ per kg of styrene of reaction enthalpy alone, before the steam dilution the process runs on - so the template's 2.0 MJ is certainly too low, which is precisely why heat and electricity are left free. The reason to do this one first: styrene is the monomer of GPPS, HIPS, SAN, ABS and the already-committed expandable-polystyrene spec, and 'Ethyl benzene, at plant' is a real 7-input BAFU unit process, so the chain terminates on the database. One level further down, ethylbenzene's own benzene input is itself an unflagged eco-profile whose rebuild is blocked by the BTX allocation - that is a finding about the database, not something this spec can fix.

Transport, freight, lorry, fleet averageRERcalibrated
The dataset's own text names 'transportation of feedstock' but no report in the bundle gives a distance for it, so the amount is calibrated. The starting value is the ecoinvent v2 standard distance for precursor materials (Althaus 2007 sec. 34.6.7: 'As there is no information about the transport amounts, standard distances and means according to Frischknecht et al. 4.53e-10kilowatt hour0%2.02e-10
Chemical plant, organicsRERcalibrated · at lower bound
Rajabihamedani 2025 sec. 3.6: 'For the implementation, the dataset "Chemical plant, organics {RER}U" is used. The quantity is the standard amount for production of chemicals of 4.0E-10 p.' The target carries exactly that, which is why 4.0E-10 is the upper bound here. It is the upper bound and not the amount because ESU added one chemical plant per *dataset*, i.e. one for a whole cradle-to-gate chain, while this model links to a precursor dataset that already carries its own 4.0E-10 - so a second one is a double count against the target's own convention. That matters more than its size suggests: of the 1790 flows in the cumulative vector of every one of these 14 datasets, 1316 get more than 90 % of their value from this single exchange (see scripts/plasticseurope_infrastructure_diagnostic.py), so doubling it doubles three quarters of the vector. The amount is therefore fitted between 0 and the report's 4.0E-10.

Evidence

  • 2025 - LCI plastics - Rajabihamedani.pdf (the dataset's own cited report) — sec. 3.2, Tab. 1.1, sec. 6
    Prints no unit-process inventory for any of its 14 datasets: 'All datasets addressed for the updates are nowadays only available as system processes and therefore are modelled accordingly in this study' (sec. 3.2); Foreground data = 'Cumulative inventories of Plastics as provided by PlasticsEurope' (Tab. 1.1). Verified page by page, including every table that is embedded as an image. The full finding is in specs/evidence/<code>/response-0-locate.json. What the report does give and is used here: sec. 3.6 (infrastructure, 4.0E-10 p of Chemical plant, organics).
  • bafu-2026 ecoSpold metadata of the target (includedProcesses / technology) — includedProcesses / technology
    'The VCM production process, upstream processes like ethylene and chlorine production, electricity, and steam production as well as transportation of feedstock and waste treatment.' - this is the whole input list the dataset declares, and it is what the model contains.
  • 2007 - LCI chemicals - Althaus.pdf — sec. 34.5.3 Thermal cracking of EDC
    'C2H4 + Cl2 -> C2H4Cl2 (Chlorination of ethylene to EDC) / C2H4Cl -> CH2CHCl + HCl (Cracking of EDC to form VCM) / C2H4 + 1/2 O2 + 2HCl -> C2H4Cl2 + H2O (Oxychlorination route to EDC)'. 'Often all the HCl generated in the cracking section is reused in producing EDC by oxychlorination. Plants that exhibit this characteristic and also do not export EDC are called “balanced”. The balanced process is the common process used as a Best Available Technology benchmark.' Summing the three gives the balanced overall reaction 2 C2H4 + Cl2 + 1/2 O2 -> 2 C2H3Cl + H2O, in which the HCl cancels - which is why VCM is modelled here from ethylene and chlorine directly and not from 1.583 kg of EDC (that route would double-count the chlorine, because half of it comes back as HCl).
  • 2007 - LCI chemicals - Althaus.pdf — sec. 34.5.1 EDC by direct chlorination of ethylene
    'Yield on ethylene 96-98% / on chlorine 98%' - the midpoint 97 % and the 98 % are applied to the stoichiometric amounts.
  • bafu-2026: Ethylene dichloride, at plant [RER] — cross-check of the transport convention
    The one BAFU unit process for the same chemistry carries 0.6 tkm rail + 0.1 tkm lorry per kg, chlorine, liquid, production mix 0.735 kg and ethylene 0.295 kg - it reproduces Althaus Tab. 34.3 exactly, which is the check that this way of reading the report gives back the dataset it built.

Rebuilt · S3
6 inputs

NOT S1, and not complete either - read this before using the node. No report in the bundle prints an inventory. The dataset's ecoSpold technology field names both monomers - 'production out of hexamethylene diamine and adipic acid' - and the polycondensation equation gives both amounts exactly: Polycondensation: C6H10O4 (adipic acid, 146.142) + C6H16N2 (hexamethylenediamine, 116.208) -> -[C12H22N2O2]- (nylon-6,6 repeat unit, 226.320) + 2 H2O (36.030); 262.350 = 262.350. Per kg of nylon 6,6: 146.142/226.320 = 0.64573 kg of adipic acid and 116.208/226.320 = 0.51347 kg of hexamethylenediamine, releasing 0.15921 kg of water. Molar masses from the IUPAC 2021 standard atomic weights: H 1.008, C 12.011, N 14.007, O 15.999, S 32.06, Cl 35.45, Na 22.990. Only one of the two can be linked: 'Adipic acid, at plant' [RER] is a real 7-input BAFU unit process, but BAFU-2026 contains no hexamethylenediamine dataset at all, and no adiponitrile or hexanedinitrile either - searched by name over all 11,947 datasets. So this model carries 0.646 kg of the 1.159 kg of monomer the reaction needs, i.e. 56 % of the input mass and none of the nitrogen; the missing 0.513 kg of diamine is not a rounding gap but half the chemistry, and it is why the flow agreement of this node must not be read as a quality statement about the method. It is published rather than withheld because the adipic-acid half is exact and because the missing half is named, quantified and traceable: anyone who adds a hexamethylenediamine dataset can complete it by adding one line.

Rebuilt · S2
1 inputs
0 direct flows
S2, not S1: no report in the bundle prints an inventory for the APME mix (the locate response records the search). What makes this one reachable anyway is that BAFU-2026 already contains a unit process of the same product at the same location - 'Naphtha, at refinery' [RER], 33 technosphere inputs and 126 elementary flows, the ecoinvent refinery model documented in 2007 - Crude oil - Jungbluth.pdf. The two datasets differ in provenance, not in product: the target is PlasticsEurope's own cumulated refinery figure for the naphtha its members buy ('product out of refining process of oil and gas', ecoSpold technology field), the sibling is ecoinvent's model of the same European refinery slate. Substituting one for the other is exactly the template transfer S2 describes, and it is a 1:1 substitution because both are 1 kg of naphtha at the refinery gate - there is no region, scale or vintage to adapt, so nothing is calibrated and there is no free amount in this spec at all. What the check then measures is a real question about the database: how far apart are PlasticsEurope's and ecoinvent's naphtha? Caveat: the target is the APME *mix*, which may be sourced differently from the ecoinvent RER slate, and nothing in the bundle says how. AFTER THE CHECK. The question this spec was written to answer - how far apart are PlasticsEurope's and ecoinvent's naphtha? - has an answer, and it is: far. Fossil CO2 agrees to -16 %, but every other headline flow of the APME profile is far higher than the ecoinvent refinery model produces: methane -98.9 %, NMVOC -98.3 %, nitrogen oxides -84.8 %, carbon monoxide -82.3 %, sulfur dioxide -74.7 %. In other words the APME naphtha carries roughly 50-100x the methane and NMVOC of ecoinvent's, at comparable CO2. Overall: 0 of the 50 largest kilogram flows within +-10 %, 4 of 1668 determined flows. The two datasets are not interchangeable, and this spec's value is that it says so with numbers rather than leaving the substitution to be made silently downstream.

Rebuilt · S3

NOT S1. The cited report prints no inventory. Althaus 2007 ch. 96 is about this very dataset name, but its inventory tables 96.5-96.7 are the *cumulative* vector of the PlasticsEurope module - the report says so: 'Due to the fact that this dataset is cumulated it was not possible to use the other processes modelled in ecoinvent to obtain a transparent process chain' - so they are the aggregate this project is trying to open, not a unit process. What ch. 96 does print, from the EU BREF (European Commission 2002), is a gate-to-gate picture of a reformate xylene plant as ranges: Tab. 96.1 consumptions and yields, Tab. 96.2 air emissions, Tab. 96.3 wastewater, Tab. 96.4 solid wastes. That is what is modelled here. Every free amount carries bounds taken straight from those printed ranges - none of them was chosen to make the agreement look better, and the feedstock bounds in particular are very wide (1.37-4.35 kg per kg) because the printed yield range is. Same caveat as Althaus's own: 'Within the module assessed here there are only the resources and emissions considered which are given in the data source. Therefore no land use could be included and no direct soil emissions within the process chain are stated.'

Chemical plant, organicsRERcalibrated · at lower bound
Rajabihamedani 2025 sec. 3.6: 'For the implementation, the dataset "Chemical plant, organics {RER}U" is used. The quantity is the standard amount for production of chemicals of 4.0E-10 p.' The target carries exactly that, which is why 4.0E-10 is the upper bound here. It is the upper bound and not the amount because ESU added one chemical plant per *dataset*, i.e. one for a whole cradle-to-gate chain, while this model links to a precursor dataset that already carries its own 4.0E-10 - so a second one is a double count against the target's own convention. That matters more than its size suggests: of the 1790 flows in the cumulative vector of every one of these 14 datasets, 1316 get more than 90 % of their value from this single exchange (see scripts/plasticseurope_infrastructure_diagnostic.py), so doubling it doubles three quarters of the vector. The amount is therefore fitted between 0 and the report's 4.0E-10.

Evidence

  • 2025 - LCI plastics - Rajabihamedani.pdf (the dataset's own cited report) — sec. 3.2, Tab. 1.1, sec. 6
    Prints no unit-process inventory for any of its 14 datasets: 'All datasets addressed for the updates are nowadays only available as system processes and therefore are modelled accordingly in this study' (sec. 3.2); Foreground data = 'Cumulative inventories of Plastics as provided by PlasticsEurope' (Tab. 1.1). Verified page by page, including every table that is embedded as an image. The full finding is in specs/evidence/<code>/response-0-locate.json. What the report does give and is used here: sec. 3.6 (infrastructure, 4.0E-10 p of Chemical plant, organics).
  • bafu-2026 ecoSpold metadata of the target (technology) — technology
    'Xylenes are commercially produced based on two different feedstocks: - Pyrolysis gasoline, a side product of thermal cracking (steam cracking) of hydrocarbons (e.g. naphtha, gas oil, ethane, propane, butanes, natural gas liquids) - Reformate, a product of catalytic reforming of naphtha. ... In Europe (EU27) xylenes are mainly produced via pyrolysis gasoline (14 %) and reformate (86 %).' The 14/86 split is the only quantitative statement about this dataset anywhere, and it fixes the feedstock mix.
  • 2007 - LCI chemicals - Althaus.pdf — Tab. 96.1 (printed p. 854, PDF p. 935)
    'Energy usage for and precursor materials for xylene production (European Commission (2002))', column 'Reformate plant', per ton of feedstock: Fuel gas 3 - 10 kg; Steam (tons) 0.5 - 1.5; Electricity (MWh) <0.07. Production (tons) per ton of feedstock: p-xylene 0.23 - 0.48; o-xylene 0 - 0.25. Summing the two product ranges gives 0.23 - 0.73 t of xylenes per t of feedstock, i.e. 1.370 - 4.348 kg of feedstock per kg of product, and every consumption range is multiplied by that to get a per-kg-of-product range.
  • 2007 - LCI chemicals - Althaus.pdf — Tab. 96.2 and Tab. 96.3 (same page), row 'BTX from aromatic mixture (source: a single plant)'
    Air, kg/ton product: Methane 0.086; SO2 0.53; Particulates 0.008 kg/ton *feedstock*; VOC 0.03 toluene and 0.2 NMVOCtot. Water, kg/ton product: Benzene 0.003; Toluene 0.001; COD 0.087; N-Kjeldahl 0.0009. Two rows are not used: the particulates, because they are per ton of feedstock and the feedstock amount here is itself calibrated, and the second 'Methane' column (0.09), because the table has two columns with that same header and the report gives no way to tell what the second one is. The caption reads 'Air emissions for toluene production' inside the xylenes chapter, which is a copy-paste slip in the report; the rows are the BTX plant the chapter is about.
  • 2007 - LCI chemicals - Althaus.pdf — Tab. 96.4 and sec. 96.5 Solid wastes
    kg/ton product: Catalysts 0.05 'recycled via supplier'; Clay, desiccant material, inert balls 0.006 'reused after regeneration'; Activated carbon 0.01 'incinerated'; Filter cloth etc. 0.0009 'incinerated'. The first two leave the system as recovered material and are skipped; the two incinerated streams are modelled together, since 'The wastes are usually recycled, landfilled or incinerated in a hazardous waste incinerator.'

Polystyrene, high impact, HIPS, at plant · RER · per 1 kilogram
  • 45.2 megajoule Crude Oil Resources from ground

Rebuilt · S3
7 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory for this dataset. The dataset's own ecoSpold technology field is 'polymerization out of ethylene and benzene by free radical processes' - word for word the same text as the general-purpose grade, so the field does not distinguish HIPS from GPPS at all and gives no rubber. What does distinguish them is the product name: high-impact polystyrene is polystyrene toughened with polybutadiene rubber, which is why BAFU carries a separate dataset for it and why PlasticsEurope's own 2012 eco-profile for it is titled 'High impact polystyrene (HIPS); polymerisation of polybutadiene' (quoted in Tab. 6.1 of 2025 - LCI plastics - Rajabihamedani.pdf, which lists it as 'Recommended to be added' and prints no inventory). So the structure - styrene plus polybutadiene - is evidence-backed; the split is not. No number for the rubber content exists in the bundle, so both monomers are free and constrained only by mass_sum = 1.0 kg: addition polymerisation loses no mass, so whatever is not styrene is rubber. The split is therefore a fit, not a measurement, and is flagged low confidence. This is the same device the committed cement ZN spec uses for a composition given only as ranges. AFTER THE FIT - READ THIS BEFORE USING THE AMOUNTS. The calibration was run and its result is structurally wrong, which is worth more than the spec was: the fit put 0.9998 kg on polybutadiene and 1.0E-4 kg on styrene - high-impact *polystyrene* modelled as pure rubber, where the real rubber content is a few per cent. The inventory agreement it buys is 621 of 1668 determined flows within +-10 % (37 %), median |delta| 11.3 %. This is the identifiability trap the project's own calibration benchmark predicts (README sec. 6: 'a better inventory fit with the wrong structure'), caught here on real data rather than on a synthetic case. The mechanism is specific and repeatable: under a mass constraint, when one candidate monomer is another dataset of the same APME eco-profile family and another is an ecoinvent unit process, the fit will empty the ecoinvent one, because it drags in a full background of ~1670 elementary flows that the target's ~145-substance eco-profile simply does not have, and every one of them counts against the fit. And among same-family candidates the fit follows the waste-treatment exchanges, not the chemistry - which is why it prefers polybutadiene to styrene: 'Styrene, at plant' declares 0.5615 kg of hazardous waste to incineration per kg, 80x what its own polymer GPPS declares (0.0070 kg) and 250x its own feedstock benzene (0.0022 kg), so any node that links styrene inherits a waste chain the target does not have. Conclusion for a user: the node's *inventory* is a fair reconstruction of the target's flows, its *composition* is not a statement about the product, and the split must not be quoted. The amounts are left exactly as the fit produced them - no bound was moved after the score was seen.

Rebuilt · S3
6 inputs

NOT S1. The cited report prints no inventory and says of PTA only that a x1000 correction was applied to one land-occupation flow (sec. 3.8). The dataset's own includedProcesses field names the chemistry - 'Crude terephthalic acid (CTA) is produced by oxidation of p-xylene. A subsequent purification step leads to purified terephthalic acid (PTA)' - and the feedstock amount follows from that reaction. Two honest weaknesses: (1) the amount is the theoretical minimum, because no yield is printed anywhere in the bundle, so the model is low on p-xylene by whatever the real loss is; (2) BAFU has only 'Xylene, at plant', the mixed-isomer dataset, not p-xylene, so the isomer separation - which Althaus 2007 sec. 96.5.1 describes as crystallisation or molecular-sieve adsorption and does not quantify - is missing from the chain. The acetic acid solvent and the cobalt/manganese/bromide catalyst of the oxidation step are named by neither the dataset nor any report in the bundle and are left out rather than guessed.

Xylene, at plantRERaggregated dataset
C8H10 + 3 O2 -> C8H6O4 + 2 H2O: 106.165/166.131 = 0.6390 kg, the theoretical minimum (no yield printed in the bundle); mixed xylene stands in for p-xylene, see the strategy note
0.639kilogram99%0.667
Chemical plant, organicsRERcalibrated · at lower bound
Rajabihamedani 2025 sec. 3.6: 'For the implementation, the dataset "Chemical plant, organics {RER}U" is used. The quantity is the standard amount for production of chemicals of 4.0E-10 p.' The target carries exactly that, which is why 4.0E-10 is the upper bound here. It is the upper bound and not the amount because ESU added one chemical plant per *dataset*, i.e. one for a whole cradle-to-gate chain, while this model links to a precursor dataset that already carries its own 4.0E-10 - so a second one is a double count against the target's own convention. That matters more than its size suggests: of the 1790 flows in the cumulative vector of every one of these 14 datasets, 1316 get more than 90 % of their value from this single exchange (see scripts/plasticseurope_infrastructure_diagnostic.py), so doubling it doubles three quarters of the vector. The amount is therefore fitted between 0 and the report's 4.0E-10.

Transport, freight, lorry, fleet averageRERcalibrated
The dataset's own text names 'transportation of feedstock' but no report in the bundle gives a distance for it, so the amount is calibrated. The starting value is the ecoinvent v2 standard distance for precursor materials (Althaus 2007 sec. 34.6.7: 'As there is no information about the transport amounts, standard distances and means according to Frischknecht et al. (2007) are used for the different raw materials') = 0.6 tkm rail + 0.1 tkm lorry per kg of precursor, the ratio BAFU's Ethylene dichloride, Ethylene oxide, Ethyl benzene and PET-amorphous unit processes all carry; precursor mass here 0.639 kg. It is left free rather than fixed because the PlasticsEurope eco-profile accounts for its feedstock transport as elementary flows only - it carries no vehicle or track infrastructure - so an ecoinvent transport dataset adds a background the target cannot have, and the fit is the right place to decide how much of it belongs.

Evidence

  • 2025 - LCI plastics - Rajabihamedani.pdf (the dataset's own cited report) — sec. 3.2, Tab. 1.1, sec. 6
    Prints no unit-process inventory for any of its 14 datasets: 'All datasets addressed for the updates are nowadays only available as system processes and therefore are modelled accordingly in this study' (sec. 3.2); Foreground data = 'Cumulative inventories of Plastics as provided by PlasticsEurope' (Tab. 1.1). Verified page by page, including every table that is embedded as an image. The full finding is in specs/evidence/<code>/response-0-locate.json. What the report does give and is used here: sec. 3.6 (infrastructure, 4.0E-10 p of Chemical plant, organics).
  • bafu-2026 ecoSpold metadata of the target (includedProcesses) — includedProcesses
    'Crude terephthalic acid (CTA) is produced by oxidation of p-xylene. A subsequent purification step leads to purified terephthalic acid (PTA). In addition to these foreground processes, the following processes in the supply chain are considered for this EPD: extraction and refinery of crude oil and natural gas, steam cracking of hydrocarbons (predominantly naphtha) into lower olefins and pygas, catalytic reforming of naphtha, and the extraction and production of p-xylene from both pygas and reformate (xylene loop).'
  • stoichiometry of the p-xylene oxidation — the one material amount
    C8H10 + 3 O2 -> C8H6O4 + 2 H2O. Per kg of PTA: M(C8H10)/M(C8H6O4) = 106.165/166.131 = 0.6390 kg of p-xylene. The oxygen is taken from air and is not an inventoried input, as in every air-oxidation process in BAFU (cf. Ethylene oxide, at plant, whose oxygen is a purchased pure-O2 input and is therefore listed there but not here).
  • 2007 - LCI chemicals - Althaus.pdf — sec. 96.5.1 p-Xylene, o-Xylene
    Describes the isomer separation that this model cannot represent: 'Crystallization techniques take advantage of the spread of melting points ... p-Xylene can also be recovered using solid absorption of the molecular-sieve type.' No amounts are given for it anywhere.

Rebuilt · S3
8 inputs

NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory. The dataset's ecoSpold technology field names the complete reagent list - 'production out of phosgene, aniline and formaldehyde' - and BAFU carries a real unit process for each one ('Phosgene, liquid, at plant', 'Aniline, at plant', 'Formaldehyde, production mix, at plant'), so this model terminates on the database with no aggregated dependency. Two steps, both named by the technology field. Condensation: 2 C6H7N (aniline, 186.258) + CH2O (formaldehyde, 30.026) -> C13H14N2 (methylenedianiline, 198.269) + H2O (18.015); 216.284 = 216.284. Phosgenation: C13H14N2 + 2 COCl2 (197.820) -> C15H10N2O2 (MDI, 250.257) + 4 HCl (145.832); 396.089 = 396.089. Per kg of MDI: 186.258/250.257 = 0.74426 kg aniline, 30.026/250.257 = 0.11998 kg formaldehyde, 197.820/250.257 = 0.79046 kg phosgene; co-products 0.58273 kg HCl and 0.07199 kg water. Mass balance of the whole model: 1.6547 kg in, 1.6547 kg out. Molar masses from the IUPAC 2021 standard atomic weights: H 1.008, C 12.011, N 14.007, O 15.999, S 32.06, Cl 35.45, Na 22.990. The hydrogen chloride co-product (0.583 kg per kg of MDI, more than half the product mass) is recorded in this note and given no credit, because nothing in the bundle states an allocation. Amounts are fixed, not fitted: they are the stoichiometric floor at 100 % conversion. Known gaps: the hydrochloric acid catalyst of the condensation step and the chlorobenzene/monochlorobenzene solvent of the phosgenation, both recycled and neither quantified anywhere; and the polymeric-MDI fraction, which the dataset does not distinguish.

8.33e-12kilowatt hour0%3.71e-12
Chemical plant, organicsRERcalibrated · at lower bound
Free between zero and the template value. 4.0E-10 units is what every ecoinvent-v2 organic chemical in BAFU carries and is the upper bound; zero is the lower bound because this target declares no infrastructure exchange at all - its entire technosphere is waste-treatment services - so an eco-profile that never counted a plant is a real possibility. Both bounds come from those two facts and were set before any score was computed. BAFU template, not a report figure. Every ecoinvent-v2 organic-chemical unit process in BAFU-2026 carries the same five-line utility block: 0.333 kWh of 'Electricity, medium voltage, production ENTSO-E, at grid', 2.0 MJ of 'Heat, natural gas, at industrial furnace 1MW' [CH], 4.0E-10 units of 'Chemical plant, organics', and transport at 0.6 tkm rail + 0.1 tkm lorry per kilogram of precursor. Verified exchange by exchange on Cumene, at plant (0.684 kg benzene + 0.369 kg propylene = 1.053 kg of precursor -> 0.632 tkm rail, 0.105 tkm lorry), Ethyl benzene, at plant (1.030 -> 0.619 / 0.103), Phosgene, liquid, at plant (1.040 -> 0.624 / 0.104) and Aniline, at plant (1.352 -> 0.814 / 0.136). That is the named template this amount comes from.

Rebuilt · S3
6 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. The cited report prints no inventory. The input list is the one the BAFU dataset itself declares in its ecoSpold technology/includedProcesses field, and the monomer amount is a mass balance, not a measurement: addition (chain-growth) polymerisation adds the monomer to the chain unchanged, so the repeat unit and the monomer have the same empirical formula and 1 kg of polymer needs 1 kg of monomer plus whatever is lost. No loss figure exists anywhere in the bundle, so 1.000 kg is used and the real value can only be higher - this is the one place where the model is knowingly at a bound. Electricity and steam are named by the dataset and quantified nowhere, so they are fitted, unbounded; they are the whole gate-to-gate difference between the polymer and its monomer.

0.0098kilogram24%0.00273
Transport, freight, railRERcalibrated
The dataset's own text names 'transportation of feedstock' but no report in the bundle gives a distance for it, so the amount is calibrated. The starting value is the ecoinvent v2 standard distance for precursor materials (Althaus 2007 sec. 34.6.7: 'As there is no information about the transport amounts, standard distances and means according to Frischknecht et al. (2007) are used for the different raw materials') = 0.6 tkm rail + 0.1 tkm lorry per kg of precursor, the ratio BAFU's Ethylene dichloride, Ethylene oxide, Ethyl benzene and PET-amorphous unit processes all carry; precursor mass here 1 kg. It is left free rather than fixed because the PlasticsEurope eco-profile accounts for its feedstock transport as elementary flows only - it carries no vehicle or track infrastructure - so an ecoinvent transport dataset adds a background the target cannot have, and the fit is the right place to decide how much of it belongs.

Chemical plant, organicsRERcalibrated · at lower bound
Rajabihamedani 2025 sec. 3.6: 'For the implementation, the dataset "Chemical plant, organics {RER}U" is used. The quantity is the standard amount for production of chemicals of 4.0E-10 p.' The target carries exactly that, which is why 4.0E-10 is the upper bound here. It is the upper bound and not the amount because ESU added one chemical plant per *dataset*, i.e. one for a whole cradle-to-gate chain, while this model links to a precursor dataset that already carries its own 4.0E-10 - so a second one is a double count against the target's own convention. That matters more than its size suggests: of the 1790 flows in the cumulative vector of every one of these 14 datasets, 1316 get more than 90 % of their value from this single exchange (see scripts/plasticseurope_infrastructure_diagnostic.py), so doubling it doubles three quarters of the vector. The amount is therefore fitted between 0 and the report's 4.0E-10.

Rebuilt · S2
12 inputs
14 direct flows
The cited report prints no inventory. BAFU-2026 does carry a 16-input unit process for the same polymer in its other grade - 'Polyethylene terephthalate, granulate, amorphous, at plant' [RER] - and the target declares exactly that structure: 'The polymer process, upstream processes like pX, PTA, and MEG production, electricity, and steam production as well as transportation of feedstock and waste treatment.' Bottle grade differs from amorphous by a solid-state polycondensation step, which is extra heat and residence time and no extra material; the six energy inputs are therefore free within 0.5-2x of the template (the bound convention of the committed gypsum-fibre-board S2 spec) and the three material inputs are fixed. This is the best-evidenced of the family after ethylene glycol, and it is the only one of the fourteen whose direct process emissions are known at all - they come with the template.

    'The polymer process, upstream processes like pX, PTA, and MEG production, electricity, and steam production as well as transportation of feedstock and waste treatment.'
  • bafu-2026: Polyethylene terephthalate, granulate, amorphous, at plant [RER] (unit process, 16 inputs, 15 direct flows) — template transfer (S2)
    Per kg: purified terephthalic acid 0.875 kg, ethylene glycol 0.334 kg, nitrogen liquid 0.0298 kg, steam 0.94 kg, electricity 0.194 kWh, heat natural gas 0.665 MJ, heat heavy fuel oil 0.494 MJ, heat hard coal 0.306 MJ, heat light fuel oil 0.165 MJ, chemical plant organics 4.0E-10 unit, transport rail 0.743 tkm + lorry 0.124 tkm, four disposal services, and 15 direct elementary flows.   • stoichiometry of the PET esterification — cross-check of the two material amounts
    n HOOC-C6H4-COOH + n HO-C2H4-OH -> [-OC-C6H4-CO-O-C2H4-O-]n + 2n H2O. Per kg of repeat unit (M = 192.17): PTA 166.131/192.17 = 0.8645 kg, MEG 62.068/192.17 = 0.3230 kg. The template sits 1.2 % and 3.4 % above those minima, which is the expected order for process losses - the template is consistent with the chemistry, which is why both are kept fixed rather than fitted.
