The one case in the whole set where the answer is already inside the database. Chapter 31 of 2007 - LCI chemicals - Althaus.pdf prints two inventories of liquid epoxy resin side by side: Tab. 31.1, 'Life cycle inventory ... based on cumulative data', which is the target, and Tab. 31.2, 'Disaggregated life cycle inventory for the production of liquid epoxy resin (modified from Boustead 1999)', which is the same product as a unit process - 9 technosphere exchanges plus its own resources and direct emissions. BAFU-2026 carries both: the target, and 'Epoxy resin, liquid, disaggregated data, at plant' [RER] (code 6ac7f2be, 9 inputs, 63 elementary flows), whose exchanges reproduce Tab. 31.2 line for line - checked one by one: 2.19 kWh electricity, 11.0 MJ heavy fuel oil, 57.7 MJ natural-gas furnace, 13.0 kg tap water, 0.298 and 0.0058 kg of the two disposal services, 4.0E-10 chemical plant, 1.74 tkm rail, 60.6 tkm lorry. So this spec links that node 1:1 and calibrates nothing. A parallel spec, specs/c80b8e9e-epoxy-resin-liquid-at-plant.draft.json, transcribes Tab. 31.2 through the LLM route instead; the two should agree, and where they do not the difference is transcription or flow mapping, which is worth knowing. What the check measures here is not how good this project's method is - it is how far BAFU's own disaggregated dataset lands from BAFU's own aggregated one for the same product, which is the cleanest available answer to 'what does disaggregation cost?'. One thing to expect: the two BAFU datasets put their air emissions in different sub-compartments - the aggregated one in 'Emissions to urban air close to ground', the disaggregated one in 'Emissions to air, unspecified' - so a substance total that is exactly right can still score as two large deviations. AFTER THE CHECK. The result is the most useful number this batch produced, because it is a control with no modelling in it at all: BAFU's own disaggregated epoxy resin against BAFU's own aggregated epoxy resin, 1 kg for 1 kg. On the substances that carry the dataset it is close - fossil CO2 +1.5 %, rock salt +3.6 %, chloride to water +11.2 %, sodium to water +11.8 %, suspended solids -13.7 %, calcium +18.4 %, calcite +53.2 % - and it covers 75 % of the target's kilogram mass. On the project's headline metric it scores 6 of 1669 determined flows within +-10 %, i.e. 0 %. Both numbers are correct and they measure different things: the second is dominated by ~1500 trace flows that exist only because the two datasets reach different backgrounds, and by the sub-compartment split between them (the aggregated dataset puts its air emissions in 'Emissions to urban air close to ground', the disaggregated one in 'Emissions to air, unspecified', so a substance total that is exactly right scores as two large deviations). Anyone reading a low 'share of flows within 10 %' for this family should compare it with this row first.

S2, not S1 and not S3. The locate response records why no report route exists: Althaus Tab. 1.1 lists 'benzene, at plant RER' as 'Reported in: Part II' and the 957-page report has no benzene chapter, and what it does print about benzene - chapter 86, Tab. 86.1, 'Production (tons) per ton of feedstock: Benzene 0.12 - 0.24, Toluene 0 - 0.3' - is a multi-product BTX plant with no allocation stated. Stoichiometry does not help either: catalytic reforming is not one reaction. What does exist is a unit process of the same product elsewhere in BAFU-2026: 'Benzene, at refinery' [CH], code d0a00133, 32 technosphere inputs and 74 elementary flows - ecoinvent's refinery model, documented in 2007 - Crude oil - Jungbluth.pdf, in which benzene is a co-product of the refinery slate whose allocation ecoinvent resolved its own way. Transferring it 1:1 does not solve the allocation problem of the APME benzene; it replaces it with ecoinvent's, which at least is documented. Two caveats a user must carry: the template is CH, not RER (there is no RER variant, and a Swiss refinery is not a European average), and the two datasets are different vintages of a different industry survey. Nothing is calibrated - there is no free amount in this spec at all - so the check measures exactly one thing: how far apart PlasticsEurope's benzene and ecoinvent's benzene are. That matters beyond this row, because benzene is the feedstock of BAFU's 'Ethyl benzene, at plant' and 'Cumene, at plant', i.e. of the styrene and acetone chains rebuilt in the same batch, and they terminate on it. AFTER THE CHECK - and this is the number the spec was written to get. PlasticsEurope's benzene and ecoinvent's refinery benzene are not close: per kg of benzene the target carries 1.46 kg of fossil CO2 and the template 0.222 kg (-84.8 %), 0.0131 kg of methane against 5.7E-5 (-99.6 %), 0.0042 kg of SO2 against 1.2E-4 (-97.2 %), 0.0028 kg of NOx against 1.1E-4 (-95.9 %). A factor 6.6 on CO2 and two orders of magnitude on methane. Overall 1 of 1670 determined flows within +-10 %, 0 of the 50 largest kilogram flows. The two are different answers to the same allocation question - ecoinvent spreads the refinery's burden over the whole product slate, the APME profile charges the aromatics extraction and reforming to the aromatics - and the gap between them is roughly the size of that decision. Practical consequence: this node is a legitimate disaggregation but it is NOT a drop-in replacement, and any chain that terminates on it (ethylbenzene, cumene, and through them the styrene and acetone rebuilds of this same batch) inherits the difference. That is worth more than the score: it says the remaining gap in the BTX family is an allocation decision, not missing data.

Rebuilt · S3

NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory for this dataset. The input list comes from the dataset's own ecoSpold technology field, 'production by different types of polymerization out of MMA'. The field names the monomer outright; 'beads' is the suspension-polymerised form. Chain-growth (addition) polymerisation: the repeat unit and the monomer have the same empirical formula, so 1 kg of polymer carries 1 kg of monomer plus whatever is lost. No conversion-loss figure exists anywhere in the bundle, so 1.000 kg is used and the true value can only be higher - fixed, not fitted. Same convention as the committed HDPE / LDPE / LLDPE / PP / PVC specs. The monomer is itself an unflagged PlasticsEurope eco-profile (results/system_terminated_extended.csv, detected_by=structure), so this model terminates on an aggregated dataset and the structural check says so - correctly. It is linked to the BAFU dataset rather than to the sibling rebuild so that this spec's check measures only this spec's evidence; re-point it with the input's `sandbox` field to chain the rebuilds together.

Rebuilt · S3
8 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory. The dataset's ecoSpold technology field - 'production by emulsion polymerization out of its three monomers' - states the number of monomers, and the product name states which three: acrylonitrile, butadiene and styrene. That is the structure. No composition is printed anywhere in the bundle (the 2025 plastics report lists the 2015 PlasticsEurope ABS eco-profile in Tab. 6.1 and prints no inventory for it), so all three are free under mass_sum = 1.0 kg and the split is a fit, not a measurement - flagged low confidence. Two of the three monomers are themselves unflagged eco-profiles, so this model terminates on aggregated data and the structural check says so. AFTER THE FIT - READ THIS BEFORE USING THE AMOUNTS. The calibration was run and its result is structurally wrong, which is worth more than the spec was: the fit drove acrylonitrile - the monomer the product is named after - to 8.9E-10 kg, i.e. zero, and split the kilogram as 0.601 kg butadiene / 0.391 kg styrene, where a real ABS is roughly 20 % acrylonitrile, 20 % butadiene and 60 % styrene. The inventory agreement it buys is the best in the whole family: 1221 of 1667 determined flows within +-10 % (73 %), median |delta| 6.7 %. This is the identifiability trap the project's own calibration benchmark predicts (README sec. 6: 'a better inventory fit with the wrong structure'), caught here on real data rather than on a synthetic case. The mechanism is specific and repeatable: under a mass constraint, when one candidate monomer is another dataset of the same APME eco-profile family and another is an ecoinvent unit process, the fit will empty the ecoinvent one, because it drags in a full background of ~1670 elementary flows that the target's ~145-substance eco-profile simply does not have, and every one of them counts against the fit. And among same-family candidates the fit follows the waste-treatment exchanges, not the chemistry - which is why it prefers polybutadiene to styrene: 'Styrene, at plant' declares 0.5615 kg of hazardous waste to incineration per kg, 80x what its own polymer GPPS declares (0.0070 kg) and 250x its own feedstock benzene (0.0022 kg), so any node that links styrene inherits a waste chain the target does not have. Conclusion for a user: the node's *inventory* is a fair reconstruction of the target's flows, its *composition* is not a statement about the product, and the split must not be quoted. The amounts are left exactly as the fit produced them - no bound was moved after the score was seen.

Rebuilt · S3
6 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. The cited report prints no inventory. The input list is the one the BAFU dataset itself declares in its ecoSpold technology/includedProcesses field, and the monomer amount is a mass balance, not a measurement: addition (chain-growth) polymerisation adds the monomer to the chain unchanged, so the repeat unit and the monomer have the same empirical formula and 1 kg of polymer needs 1 kg of monomer plus whatever is lost. No loss figure exists anywhere in the bundle, so 1.000 kg is used and the real value can only be higher - this is the one place where the model is knowingly at a bound. Electricity and steam are named by the dataset and quantified nowhere, so they are fitted, unbounded; they are the whole gate-to-gate difference between the polymer and its monomer. The alpha-olefin comonomer (typically a few per cent of 1-butene or 1-hexene) is a gap: no comonomer is named in the dataset's own text and no dataset for one is identified, so it is carried inside the ethylene.

Transport, freight, railRERcalibrated
The dataset's own text names 'transportation of feedstock' but no report in the bundle gives a distance for it, so the amount is calibrated. The starting value is the ecoinvent v2 standard distance for precursor materials (Althaus 2007 sec. 34.6.7: 'As there is no information about the transport amounts, standard distances and means according to Frischknecht et al. (2007) are used for the different raw materials') = 0.6 tkm rail + 0.1 tkm lorry per kg of precursor, the ratio BAFU's Ethylene dichloride, Ethylene oxide, Ethyl benzene and PET-amorphous unit processes all carry; precursor mass here 1 kg. It is left free rather than fixed because the PlasticsEurope eco-profile accounts for its feedstock transport as elementary flows only - it carries no vehicle or track infrastructure - so an ecoinvent transport dataset adds a background the target cannot have, and the fit is the right place to decide how much of it belongs.

Chemical plant, organicsRERcalibrated · at lower bound
Rajabihamedani 2025 sec. 3.6: 'For the implementation, the dataset "Chemical plant, organics {RER}U" is used. The quantity is the standard amount for production of chemicals of 4.0E-10 p.' The target carries exactly that, which is why 4.0E-10 is the upper bound here. It is the upper bound and not the amount because ESU added one chemical plant per *dataset*, i.e. one for a whole cradle-to-gate chain, while this model links to a precursor dataset that already carries its own 4.0E-10 - so a second one is a double count against the target's own convention. That matters more than its size suggests: of the 1790 flows in the cumulative vector of every one of these 14 datasets, 1316 get more than 90 % of their value from this single exchange (see scripts/plasticseurope_infrastructure_diagnostic.py), so doubling it doubles three quarters of the vector. The amount is therefore fitted between 0 and the report's 4.0E-10.

Rebuilt · S3
7 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory. The dataset's ecoSpold technology field states the composition qualitatively - 'production by different ways out of caprolactam and filled with glass fibers' - and both components exist in BAFU: the unfilled polymer as an unflagged eco-profile and 'Glass fibre, at plant' [RER] as a real 20-input unit process. Compounding is a physical blend, so polymer + glass = 1.000 kg per kg of compound, which is imposed with mass_sum; the glass content is nowhere quantified in the bundle (commercial grades run 15-50 %), so the split is fitted, not measured, and is flagged low confidence. Note the circularity BAFU already contains and this spec inherits: 'Glass fibre, at plant' itself consumes 0.0283 kg of 'Nylon 6, at plant' as a sizing agent, so a glass-filled nylon depends on nylon twice. AFTER THE FIT - READ THIS BEFORE USING THE AMOUNTS. The fit put 0.99999 kg on the polymer and 4E-6 kg on the glass fibre, i.e. it modelled a glass-*filled* compound as unfilled polymer; commercial grades carry 15-50 % glass. The inventory agreement it buys is real (429 of 1668 determined flows within +-10 % (26 %), 85 % of the kilogram mass, median |delta| 19.9 %) and the node is a fair reconstruction of the target's flows, but its composition is not a statement about the product and must not be quoted. The mechanism is the same one recorded on the ABS, SAN and HIPS specs: under a mass constraint the fit empties whichever candidate is an ecoinvent unit process - here 'Glass fibre, at plant', a 20-input dataset that drags a full ~1670-flow background into a target whose eco-profile reports ~145 substances - and keeps the candidate that belongs to the same APME family and therefore shares the target's waste-treatment chains. No bound was moved after the score was seen.

Rebuilt · S3
6 inputs
0 direct flows
NOT S1. No report in the bundle prints an inventory. The dataset's ecoSpold technology field is 'production out of alcohols and epoxides by alkoxylation'. Alkoxylation is an addition: the epoxide opens onto the growing chain with no mass loss, so epoxide + initiator alcohol = 1.000 kg per kg of polyol. The epoxide of a polyether polyol is propylene oxide (with some ethylene oxide in tipped grades), and BAFU carries 'Propylene oxide, liquid, at plant' as a real 8-input unit process. The initiator alcohol cannot be modelled: it is glycerol, sucrose or a glycol depending on the grade, the field does not say which, and BAFU-2026 has no petrochemical glycerol dataset at all - the only glycerine datasets in it are biodiesel co-products from rape and vegetable oil, which would import an agricultural background that has nothing to do with this product, and one deprecated 'xx Glycerine, from epichlorohydrin'. So the whole 1.000 kg is charged to propylene oxide, and unlike every other stoichiometric amount in this batch that is an UPPER bound, not a lower one: the true propylene-oxide content is 1.000 kg minus the initiator, typically a few to ten per cent. It is fixed rather than fitted because there is no printed range to bound it with. The ethylene-oxide tip of the common grades is a second, unquantifiable gap.
→

Cumene, at plantRER
The only number in the whole chapter that belongs to the unit process rather than to the cumulated vector. It is internally consistent: the cumene cleavage is C6H5C(CH3)2OOH -> C6H5OH + CH3COCH3, so 1 kg of acetone (M = 58.08 g/mol) carries 120.19/58.08 = 2.07 kg of cumene at full conversion, and 2.07/0.90 = 2.30 kg at the 90 % yield the sentence states - exactly the printed 2300 kg per 1000 kg. The figure therefore assigns the whole cumene input to the acetone and none of it to the phenol, which is what the second sentence says.

Rebuilt · S3
6 inputs
0 direct flows
Labelled S3, not S1, although the cumene figure is a straight transcription - because the number that goes into the node is not the number the report prints, and that difference is the point of this spec. Chapter 3 of 2007 - LCI chemicals - Althaus.pdf, sec. 1.5.2, states: 'Wells (1999) indicates that 2300 kg of cumene are required to produce 1000 kg of acetone with a yield of 90%. The production of the co-product phenol is not included in this figure.' Those 2300 kg are the *unallocated* requirement - 120.19/58.08 = 2.07 kg of cumene per kg of acetone at full conversion, divided by the stated 90 % yield, is 2.30 kg exactly - so charging them all to the acetone gives the phenol a free ride, and the phenol is the heavier product of the two (1.62 kg per kg of acetone). This spec therefore applies mass allocation: Cumene cleavage: C6H5C(CH3)2OOH -> C6H5OH + CH3COCH3, i.e. one phenol (94.113) per acetone (58.080), so the printed 2.300 kg of cumene per kg of acetone produces 1.620 kg of phenol alongside it. Mass allocation between the two co-products gives acetone 58.080/(58.080+94.113) = 38.16 % of the cumene, i.e. 2.300 x 0.38162 = 0.87772 kg per kg of acetone. Both numbers in that product are printed: 2300 kg per 1000 kg is Wells (1999) as quoted by Althaus sec. 1.5.2, and the molar masses are standard. Molar masses from the IUPAC 2021 standard atomic weights: H 1.008, C 12.011, N 14.007, O 15.999, S 32.06, Cl 35.45, Na 22.990. A companion spec, specs/f44d9ac3-acetone-liquid-at-plant.draft.json, is the unallocated transcription produced by the LLM route, and the two are meant to be read together. Disclosure, because it matters for how much weight the agreement figures deserve: the unallocated spec was built and checked first, and its flow diff showed its cumulative fossil CO2 at 4.06 kg against the target's 1.79 kg, a factor 2.3 - which is what prompted writing the allocated variant. The allocation is a modelling decision argued from the reaction equation and stated here in full, not a bound tuned until the score improved; no amount in this spec is free except the utilities, which carry no allocation question. Known gaps: the sulphuric acid of the cleavage step, named by the chapter and never quantified; the oxygen/air of the oxidation; and the second route the chapter describes (dehydrogenation of isopropanol), which the chapter says the PlasticsEurope data does not include.

Six main amounts are fitted within bounds; three land on the 2x ceiling, i.e. the German plant is more energy-intensive than the template allows. S5 hybrid built on top.

Evidence

  • bafu-2026: Gypsum fibre board, at plant [CH] (unit process, 10 inputs) — template transfer (S2)
    Same product, Swiss unit process. The DE dataset (treeze/QualiBOB 2016, 'data from German manufacturers') keeps its board-production inventory in a confidential annex, so the CH structure is reused; grid electricity switched to DE; the six main amounts may move within 0.25-4x of the CH value. The DE dataset is per m2; the CH template (per kg) is scaled by 10 kg/m2 (Kasser 2016, D: 10 kg/m2, 12.5 mm). Free amounts bounded to 0.5-2x the scaled template.
  • 2016 - Renewal and expansion of LCA data in KBOB - Kasser.pdf — Teilbericht D Gipswerkstoffe, D.2
    10 kg/m2, 12.5 mm; board inventory confidential (Anhang Z); binder and transport inventories printed.
